Rate of interest 

13. Consequent to the increase in the Bank Rate from 
7 % ( since May 1973) to 9 % in July 1974 , thc Corporation , 
along with other all -India term lending institutions, revised 
the rates of interest charged by it on rupee loans. Accord 
ingly, the normal lending rate of interest charged by the 
Corporation from borrowing concerns was raised from 9 % 
to 10 . 25 % (net ) per annum . The rate of interest on rupee 
loans to industrial projects being set up in notified back 
ward districts / areas, subject to certain conditions, was raised 
from 7 . 5 % to 8 . 5 % ( net ) per annum . Further, the rate 
of interest on soft loans to jute industry, export oriented 
cotton textile mills , and to the hotel industry was fixed 
at 9 .25 % (net ) per annum as against 8 , 0 % per annum in 
force earlier . 


16 . The Braúch Ollice of the Corporation at Delhi was 
raised to the status of Regional Ollice with effect from 
August 6 , 1975 . A new office of the Corporation was opened 
at Nagpur with effect from January 27 , 1973. The Corpora 
lion now has 17 offices all over the country besides its l-lead 
Office . 


Brochures for Entrepreneurs 


17 . The Advisory Services Department of the Corporation 
is bringing out a series of brochures which deal with the 
various aspects of setting up medium - sized industrial projects 
So far, two brochures in the series " How to set up a medium 
sized industry " , bave been brought out viz ., ( a ) Government 
Policies and Procedures ; (b ) Guidelines for Incorporation of 
a Limited Company. A third brochure entitled " Presenting 
Your Casc for Financial Assistance" is under preparution . 
It is hoped that such brochures would be of benefit to new 
entrepreneurs, 


The revision in the rate of interest was made applicable 
to all rupce loans sanctioned op or after July 30 , 1974 as 
also to rupce loans sanctioned by the Corporation in res 
pect of which the relative loan agreements had not been 
executed before July 30 , 1974 . 


REVIEW OF OPERATIONS 1948 – 75 
18 . Starting modestly . FCI s business has grown in size 
and complexity , and its operations have progressively increas 
ed to keep pace with the tempo of industrialisation in the 
country . A signilicant feature of the Corporation s opera 
tions has been the integration of its lending and investment 
policies with the countrys Five Year Plans 


The rates of interest charged by the Corporation from 
its assisted concerns on sub - loans in foreign currencies was 
also revised during the year. The rate of interest on foreign 
currency sub - loans was raised from 9 . 5 % to 10 . 5 % (net ) 
per annum . The concessional ratc of Interest on foreign 
currency sub -loans charged to industrial projects being set 
up in notified backward districts / areas was raised by 0 . 5 % 
to 9 . 5 % (ncl ) per annum . The revised rates of interest, 
have been made applicable to all sub - loans in foreign 
currencies including sub -loans in pound sterling sanctioned 
or or after August 29 , 1974 as also to sub -loans sanctioned 
by the Corporation in respect of which loan agreements had 
not been executed by that date . 


IFCI s contribution to the economic development of the 
country should be judged not only by the amount of finan 
cial assistance sanctioned or the number of projects assisted 
by it , but also by the effoct this assistance has had on the 
economy as a whole . Some of these benefits includo the 
development of rural cconomy through assistance to indust 
rial cooperatives, especially , in the sugar and textile industries , 
devolopment of the less developed areas: increased employ 
ment opportunities; development of new products and techno 
logics ; encouragement 10 new entreprencurs and technolo 
gists ; increased availability of inputs for augmcnting agricul 
tural production , e .g . fertilisors , pesticidos , agricultural machi 
ncry ; encouragement to export-oriented and import -saving 
projects ; modernisation of export-oriented jute and textile 
mills etc . Although the small scale and ancillary industries 
have not been the direct recipients of linancial assistance 
from the Corporation , they have benefited indirectly . Finan 
cial assistance from the Corporation has helped establish # 
number of large and medium -sized industries , which havo in 
turn helped the promotion and growth of ancillary and small 
scale industries and contributed to the creation of national 
wcalth and employment . 


Co-ordination with other all-India Financial Institutions 

14 . The all -India financial institutions including the 
Corporation have evolved a common loan application forın 
for usc by eligible concerns seeking financial assistance from 
any one of them . The samc form can be used cven when 
assistance is sought jointly from more than one all-India 
financial institution . The institutions have adopted the 
concept of the lead institution whereby one of them 
takes the responsibility of arranging for a joint inspection 
of the plant site by the ofllcials of all the institutions. 
Collective discussions are arranged with the promoters as 
also among the representatives of the institutions for evolv 
ing a common approach towards various aspects of a 
project for which financial assistance is required . The 
institutions are further cxamining the feasibility of introduc 
ing a standard Loan Agreement in the case of projects 
being jointly financed by them . These steps would facilitate 
expeditious processing of applications for joint financing . 


The Corporation as on June 30, 1975 had financed 727 
large and medium sized industrial projects spread all over 
the country with a total assistance amounting to Rs. 507 .39 
Crores. This assistance was extended to 126 concerns in the 
cooperative sector, 25 concerns in the joint sector, 13 con 
cerns in the public sector and 490 in the private corporate 
sector . Disbursements amounted to Rs. 441.44 crores which 
represented 87 . 0 % of sanctions . The total assistance out 
standing as on June 30 , 1975 amounted to Rs. 235 .67 crores . 
More important, however, than tho quantum of assistance 
sanctioned by IFCI, is its catalytic role which has led to re 
Source mobilisation of the order of Rs. 2881.66 crores for 
the completion of the assisted projects . In its assistance, the 
Corporation bas covered a wide variety of industries. And , 
it is no exaggeration to say that there is hardly any industry . 
of importance in the country which has not been the benefi 
ciary of some assistance from IFCI. 


Viability Studies of High -cost Projects 


15 . As stated earlier in the Report, the stecp increase in the 
costs of trew projects has affected their commercial viability 
In order to study the impact of the rise in the project cost of 
unit of the minimum economic size , the Corporation under 
took and completed viability studies in respect of new sugar 
projects with a crushing capacity of 1,250 tonnes of cane per 
day and new cotton spinning units with 25,000 spindles . The 
Corporation is at present carrying out a similar viability study 


IFCI s operations have been consistently profitable . JFCI s 
sound financial position is reflected in the fact that it has 
been able to build up reserves, out of taxed profits which 
worc substantially in excess of the paid - up sharc capital ,

industrial cooperatives upto June 30 , 1975 , in respect of 127 
projects amounted to Rs. 115. 86 crores which represented 
22. 8 % of the total sanctions by the Corporation since its 
inception . The rupee loan assistance sanctioned to coopera . 
tives accounted for 32.8 % of the total rupeo loan sanctioned . 
Disbursement of assistance to cooperatives aggregated Rs.

New projects claimed 81. 3 % of the total net financial

the balance of 18 .7 % of the assistance was sanctioned to ex 
pansion projects. Financial assistance sanctioned by the 
Corporation represented 30 .9 % of the cost of new projects 
and 39 .4 % of the cost of expansion projects .

24 . The extent to which saving in the agricultural sector 
have oceu mobiilsed for productive purposes as a result of the 
catalylic role of the Corporation is evident from the fact that 
in respect of 77 cooperative sugar factories in operation 
during the 1973- 74 scason , grower member and other had 
contributed a sum of Rs. 41, 75 crores as share capital while 
contribution from the State Governments amounted to Rs. 
22 . 14 crores. In addition, assisted sugar cooperatives which 
had gone into production had mobilised refundable and non 
Tetundable deposits , internal accruals etc ., amounting to Rs. 
12. 46 crores . The assistance sanctioned by tho Corporation 
for the projects of these cooperatives amounted to 47. 3 % 
of the total project cost of Rs, 160 . 55 crores . The assistance 
in the form of long tețm loans extended by the Life Insu 
rance Corporation of India to these projects amounted to 
Rs. 8 . 25 crores and represented 5 . 1 % of the total project 
cost. 

25. As stated last year in our Report, the cost of setting 
up a sugar factory with a crushing capacity of 1, 250 tonnes 
per day has been increasing significantly . The cost was Rs. 
226 lakhs in 1969, Rs, 360 lakhs in 1973 and Rs. 530 lakhs 
in early 1974 and is now of the order of Rs. 700 lakhs. 
The implications of this development from the point 
of view of profitability and debt service capacity of such 
high cost units in the country were examined in the Corpo 
ration and brought to the notice of Government, Govern 
mont thereafter appointed a Committeo (called the Sampat 
Committee ) to examine the question of the economic viability 
of new sugar projects in the light of the increased capital 
cost. Tho Committed has submitted its report and its ro 
commendations are under consideration of the Government. 

In view of the steep increase in the capital cost of new 
sugar projects and in the context of the constraint on the 
Corporation in regard to resources , the Corporation is no 
longer in a position to meet the higher loan requirements 
entirely from its own resources. It has now become neces 
sary to adopt a consortium approach whereby the other all 
India financial institutions, besides the Life Insurance Corpo 
ration of India which has been extended term loans for 
sugar projects, also meet a part of the loan requirements of 
new sugar projects , 

THE CORPORATE SECTOR 
26 . The financial assistance sanctioned to tho corporate 
sector amounted to Rs. 391. 53 crores in respect of 600 pro 
jects as on June 30 , 1973 . Details of assistance sanctioned 
to the corporate sector arc reviewed in the following para

BENEVOLENT RESERVE FUND 
32 . The IFC Act, 1948, as amended in December 1972 , 
provided for the creation of the Benevolent Reserve Fund . 
With the creation of the Benevolent Reserve Fund two years 
ago it has been possible for the Corporation to undertake 
various promotional activitics . As provided in Section 32B 
of the IFC Act, the Fund is being utilised for ( a ) meeting the 
cost of feasibility studies , project reports, market and techno 
economic surveys , (b ) assisting projects promoted by new 
entrepreneurs and technologists, (c ) promoting rescarch in 
financial and industrial management by creation of chairs in 
Universities and other academic institutions, ( d ) meeting the 
cost of training of personnel of financial institutions, etc . 
Since the creation of the Benevolent Reserve Fund a sum of 
Rs. 80 . 00 lakhs has been transferred to the Fund out of the 
profits of the Corporation . A further sum of Rs, 31.00 lakhs 
is being transferred to the Fund out of the current years 
profits of the Corporation , 


33 . The creation of the Benevolent Reserve Fund has given 
a new dimension to the promotional activities of the Corpo 
ration . Some of the activities undertaken by the Corporation 
prider the Fund are reviewed in the following paragraphs . 
Industriul Potential Survey Reports 

34. The Corporation has participated in surveys of less 
developed States /Union Territories , sponsored by the Indus 
trial Development Bank of India ; with a view to assessing 
this industrial potential and for identifying projects which 
could be implemented over the short term . These surveys 
have been completed for all the 18 States / Union Territories 
identified as backward by the Pande Committee / Planning 
Commission viz ., Andaman & Nicobar Islands, Andhra Pra 
desh , Arunachal Pradesh , Assam , Bihar, Dadra and Nagar 
Haveli , Goa , Daman and Diu , Jammu and Kashmir , Hima 
chal Pradesh , Lakshadweep, Madhya Pradesh , Manipur, Nagu 
land, Orissa , Pondicheșry , Rajasthan, Tripura and Uttar 
Pradesh ,

615 


While the Corporation is following a policy of encouray 
ing new cntrepreneurs to set up industries , there are practical 
difficulties in making this policy truly effective. One of the 
inain problems faced by the new entrepreneurs and techno 
logists intending to promote industrial projects is the lack of 
resources to be contributed by them as promoters cquiły . 


40 . The question of providing supplementary finance to 
wards promoters equity had been engaging the attention of 
the Corporation for quite some time. This issue was also 
examined by the Committeo on Development of Small and 
Medium Entreprenours set up by Government in 1973 . One 
of the recommendations of the Committee was that the finan 
cial institutions should not be too rigid in insisting on a 
minimum share capital from an entrepreneur and should be 
prepared to help him to contribute his sharo capital and hold 
it in trust for him till such time as he is in a position to buy 
it back . Government has accepted this recommondation in 
principle, subject, however , to the availability of adequate 
resources and submission of well- conceived and viable 
schemes. 


41. Recognising the imperative ikeed to assist in all possible 
ways, the new cntrants into industry , so that the national ob 
jective of broadening the entrepreneurial base in industry is 
achieved , the Corporation sponsored during the year the 
Risk Capital Foundation as a Society registered under the 
Societies Registration Act, 1860 . The Foundation which 
would be located in New Delhi was inaugurated by Shri 
T . A . Pai, Minister of Industry and Civil Supplies on March 
10 , 1975 . 


42. The main objective of the Foundation as cnvisaged 
would be to provide assistance to now entrepreneurs, parti 
cularly technologists and professionals , for promoting indus 
trial projects, by giving soft loans to enable them to provide 
a part of the equity capital as promoters contribution in res 
nect of projects which have been sanctioned financial assis 
tance by the Industrial Finance Corporation of India , alone 
or jointly with other all-India financial institutions . 


The survey reports, which have been printed , have becii 
discussed with the concerned officials of the State Govern 
ments and representatives of the State - level financial institu 
tions and other industrial interests and projects ideas that 
seem prima facic viablo have been identilled , follow -up 
action on the survey reports is being taken by Inter - Institu 
tional Groups ( IIGS) established in the various States . The 
Corporation is taking an active interest in the functioning of 
the IIGS. 
Commissioning of Feaslbillty Studles " . 

35. Another important promotional measure undertaken hy 
the term lcriding institutions is the commissioning of feasibi 
lity reports on candidate projects identified in the afore -men 
tioned industrial potential surveys. The cost of the feasibility 
reports is being shared among these institutions. These 
reports are commissioned with a view to attracting the atten 
tion of intending investors to the possibilities of the various 
projects. As on Juno 30 , 1975 , 18 feasibility reports hadi 
heen commissioned and these covered a Sugar Mill, a Hotel 
Project, a Jute Mill, a Steel Billets plant, a Granulated 
Fortiliser plant , a Solvent Extraction Unit , a Vegetable and 
Fruit Processing Unit, Central Processing and Powerloons 
Projects ( 3 Reports ), a Pulp and Paper Mill , a Calciun Car 
bide Project, a Calcium Carbonate and Hydrated Lime Pro 
inct, a Cold Storage plant for Potatoes, an Asbestos Cement 
Sheets plant, a Meat Processing plant and tanncry for hides 
and skins, a $ al sced Oil Project and a Cement Project, 
Technical Assistance Scheme 

36 . The Corporation instituted a Technical Assistance 
Scheme last year. Under this Scheme, every month , two offi 
cials at the middle and upper levels of the State -level finuncial 
an developmental agencies are invited to visit the Head 
Office of the Corporation and its Regional Office in New 
Delhi for a period of one month . The Scheme also envisages 
visits of chief executives of the State -level financial and deve 
lopmental agencies for a brief period to the Head Office of 
the Corporation . This Scheme gives an opportunity to the 
Corporation to share its experience in development banking 
with the officers of the State -level developmental agencies and 
also gives these officials an opportunity to study the policies 
procedures nd practices of the Corporation . 
Creation of Chairs 

37 . In order to promote research in developinent banking 
and in financial and industrial management, the Corporation 
has created Chairs in the Indian Institute of Management, 
Ahmedabad and the University of Delhi by giving cndow 
ment of Rs. 7 .00 lakhs and Rs. 8 .00 laklıs respectively . The 
Indian Institute of Management, Ahmedabad has already 
appointed the IFCI Professor of Management and the Uni 
versity of Delhi expects to find a suitable person for the 
Chair in the near future. 

· RISK CAPITAL FOUNDATION 
38 . In recent years, with a view to enlarging the entre 
preneurial base in industry , Government has been laying in 
creasing emphasis on encouragement to new entrepreneurs , 
particularly technologists and profossionals, intending to 
start small and medium scale industrios. In February 1973 , 
Government effocted certain changes in their Industrial 
Licensing Policy in order to prevent the cntry of large under 
takings into areas that are primarily nicant for small , modiun ) 
and now entrepreneurs . Further , the concept of joint sector 
was spelt out to nurture the growth of new entrepreneurs 
through participation of State Governments / State -level înşti 
tutions in the promotion of industrial projects in partnership 
with new entrepreneurs . 

39 . The Corporation , in its operations, has been endeavour 
ing to give practical shape to this declared policy of the 
Government by giving encouragement to new entrepreneur s 
and technologists to set up sound and viablo industrial pro 
jects. Apart from giving financial assistance , the Corporation 
iş oxtonding help and guidance to the new entrepreneurs and 
technologists la project preparation , The Corporation s policy 
is not to reject projects which initially do not meet its tests 
of viahility but to try to improve their viability by suggesting 
necessary modifications and help fill the gaps in project plan 
ning which might come to its notice . The new entrepreneurs 
who receive special attention from the Corporation are thus 
assured of rocossary support in planning and formulation of 
their projects,

( hy ) Amounts received for the purposes of the Founda

The Corporation has approved an initial allocation of

Foundation to enable it to start functioning. Further alloca 
tions will be made by the Corporation from time to time. 


Managentent Development Institute 


44. It may be recalled that the Corporation sponsored the 
Management Development Institute (MDD) in 1973 with the 
twin objectives of (a ) providing training in modern manage 
ment techniques to the personnel of assisted concerns of the 
Corporation , particularly to new entrepreneurs and technolo 
gists , who, for the first time, promote an industrial project 
with financial assistance from the Corporation (MDI has, 
since extended its services also to other concerns in the pri 
vate , joint and public sectors ) and (b ) imparting training in 
development banking to the staff of the financial institutions 
at the State and all - India levels . 


45 . MDI started conducting courses from March 1974 . Till 
December 31, 1974 , MDI had organised 14 courses in fields 
like general management, personnel management, marketing 
management, financial management develonment banking and 
in the area of management of specific industries besides one 
programme for the training of the Corporation s staff . In 
all, 424 participants took part in the 14 programmes which 
were held in different parts of the country.

" IPIIP PROGRAMMES 
50 . Public sector enterprises have been assigned a crucial 
rolo in the industrial development of the country . Further , 
the joint sector concept has given a new dimension to the 
process of industrialisation in the country . These develop 
ments have placed increasing responsibilities on the officials 
of the State Governments and the State - level developmental 
agencies, engaged as they are in the promotion of medium 
and large industrial projects in the public and the joint sec 
tors, They are also closely connected with the projects be 
ing implemented by entrepreneurs on their own , as the latter 
would have to depend on the State -level financial institutions 
for guidance and financial assistance , as also on the Govern 
mont for various approvals, incentives, etc . Further, the 
State Governments as well as the Industrial Development 
Corporations at the State - level are also engaged , in soinc 
cases, in the actual management and vpertaion of industrial 
enterprises set up by them , 


The second programme on developinent banking entitled 
" General Course on Develonment Banking" was designed for 
middle level executives of the State and all - India financial 
institutions and commercial hanks. The main focus of this 
corne was to eonid tho participants with modern techniques 
of project appraisal and project supervision and to provide 
them with a hotter understanding of the various tasks being 
Lindertaken by development banks. 

The third programme in the series was on " Legal Aspects 
of Development Banking” . This course provided an oppor 
tunity to the legal officers of the financial institutions at the 
State and all- India levels and also cominercial hanks to dis 
c .194 in depth the problems arising in term lending pronosi 
tions and to share their experiences . MDI also organised 
other courses relating to development banking like tho " Role 
of Nominec Directors" and " Rehabilitation of Sick Projects," 


51. The Corporation felt that a better understanding of the 
various aspects of project identification , formulation, evalua 
tion and implementation of industrial projects on the part 
of the officials at the State -level would greatly facilitate the 
implementation of industrial development programmes of the 
States . At present, there is no agency in tho country which 
has organised a comprehensive programme covering these 
various aspects of project development and implementation 
as also the operational problems involved . With this in view . 
the Corporation decided to sponsor programincs on " Identi 
fication , Promotion and Implementation of Industrial Pro 
jects" . (IPIIP ) for the benefit of officers in the State Govern 
monts and Stato -lovel developmental agencies as also entre 
preneurs. These programmes aro being conducted on behalf 
of tho Corporation by tho Management Developinent Insti 
tute . The first sich programme was organised at Pori in 
Orissa in February this year. There were 43 participants, 
including nominees of the Orissa State Government and the 
State -level developmental agencies . The programme covered 
aspects like project promotion , financing of projects, project 
appraisal. imnlementation and management of projects. It is 
pronoard to hold the next PIIP proramo nt Shillong for 
th . North - eastern States from October 3 , 1975 to October ? 
1973 . Similar programmes in other rarts of the country will 
he held in the coming months in conguiltation with the State 
Governments concerned .

Agricultural Tractors 

There are 10 units manufacturing agricultural tractors in 
the range of 25 HP to 75 HP with a total installed capacity 
of 48 ,000 nos. The production of tractors stood at 29 ,052 
during 1974 , 

The litilisation of capacity of one of our assisted concerns 
was only 57 % due to power shortage and shortage of raw 
materials, such as alloy steel. Another concern which is in 
the State sector having complete indigenous design and know 
how started commercial production in April 1974 . Its capa

shortage of raw materials such as steel, forgings , castings, 
engines etc . Both the concerns experienced difficulty in get 
ting tyres and tubes.

56 . From the date the agreement is executed and so lang 
as any part of the loan remains outstanding, the Corporation 
carries out Slie inspections poin during the construction and 
Opciauon periods of the project and inspects DUOKS OL durvuilt 
OL me assisted concerns . Such inspections are carried out 
generally by teams of inancial and technical officers of the 
Corpo acion . A reasonabie notice of about 10 / 15 days is 
ordmanly given to an assisted concern of the proposcu ils 
pection and for its preparing and furnishing ine parucwars 
and data for inspecuon . 10 facilitate proper inspection the 
assisted conceins are required to maintain records showing 
inc expeudicure incurred on the project, utilisation of the 
disbursements out of IFCI loan , progress of the project and 
ihe operations and financial working of the company. in 
carrying out technical and financial inspections, the omncers of 
the Corporation visit the borrowei s factory , examine relevant 
records and accounts and also schedules, cost esulmates , plans 
and speciucations of the plant, etc . In these inspecuons, em 
phasis is more on discussing the matters and affairs personally 
with the concerned officials of the company and seeking 
necessary clarifications from them . 


Further, the inspection team satisfies itself that the princi 
pal sum of loan received from the Corporation is kept in a 
separate bank account , and strictly utilised for the purpose 
for which it has been sanctioned . All releases of the loan 
amount are preceded or followed by a physical verification 
of the utilisation of the loan amount already disbursed and 
the progress made by the assisted concern towards the im 
plementation of the project . 


Where other public financial institutions are also involved , 
reports in regard to periodical inspections, unless carried out 
joutly , are mutually exchanged . Reports are forwarded in 
ine case of sub -loans in foreign currencies to the respectly . 
foreign financial institutions, wherever necessary . 


Apart from obtaining periodical progress reports and 
carrying out of inspections, the assisted concerns are also 
required to send the annual audited balance sheet and cir 
cuiars and minutes of share-holders meetings to the Cor 
poration . The financial statements are carefully studied and 
analysed to assess their progress , profitability and other finan 
cial aspects compared with the performance of at least ine 
preceeding 3 years. In such an examination apart from draw 
ing conclusions in regard to the overall performance of the 
company, the unusual features or breach of covenants with 
IFCI, if any, are also examined .

wuceras generally under the following circumstances :

The persons nominated as directors are encher IFCI s own

I ne persons nominated as directors by IFCI hold office 
during its pieasure and are not liable to hold any quaiincauon 
shaies or 10 reurement by rotation . The nominee directors 
illo expected to take active part in all deliberations of the 
Duard meetings of the assisted concerns. Without interfering 
But ine day- to -day management, the nominee directors are 
expected to participate in the discussions on all matters com 
ing up at tne Board meetings, specially those which have a 
bearing on the assistance given by IFCI and affect its inte 
rests or are otherwise important as matters of public policy . 

To enable the nominee directors to keep themselves in 
touch with the operations of the concern in matters like pro 
uuction in relation to installed capacity , sales , reasonableness 
of inventories and receivables , liquidity of the concern , meet 
ing of statutory obligations, changes in key personnel etc ., 
proforma have been devised on industry -wise basis for facili 
tating reporting of certain information and operational data 
by the companies for consideration at every meeting of the 
Board of Directors. 
Conversion of Loans into Equity 

59. In accordance with the guidelines issued by Govern 
ment, the Corporation is reserving the right of conversion or 
a part of the loans given by it into equity capital of the 
assisted concerns and the rationale behind this policy has now 
gained fuller acceptance . The Corporation , as on June 30 , 
1975 , had stipulated the conditions relating to conversion o . 
rupee loans into equity in respect of 180 concerns covering 
214 cases of sanction ( loan agreements executed in respect or 
142 cases ) after prior negotiations with the sponsors of the 
projects and in consultation with IDBI. The conversion right 
was actually exercised in the case of one concern as on June 
30 , 1975 ; in the case of others, either the time for the exer 
cise of the right had not yet arrived or it was considered 
expedient taking into account all relevant factors, to wait for 
some time more. 

It will, therefore, be seen that the relationship between a 
financial institution and an assisted concern is a business 
relationship and it is but natural that the Corporation shou. u 
take steps not only to protect and safeguard its interests, but 
also to offer advisory services . The Corporation , because of 
its considerable financial stake becomes a partner in the 
enterprise and is , therefore , deeply interested in its operations . 

The follow - up procedures call for information which any 
prudent management would collect and study in its own 
interest. As such , the follow -up procedures are not burden 
some but, on the contrary , are an aid to efficient manage 
ment. 
Progress of Repayments 

60 . The number of defaulting concerns in the engineering 
group of industries was 40 as against 26 in the previous year. 
Shortage of power continued to be the main factor afflicting 
the working of these units. Some of the units also faced 
shortage of aluminium , the production of which was itself 
affected by power shortage. Other factors which contributed 
to the unsatisfactory position in the engineering industry 
were rising costs of production due to inflationary conditions, 
inefficient management in some cases and difficult working 
capital position . During the latter part of 1974, some of the 
units also faced market resistance caused by the rise in sell 
ing prices and the credit squeeze . 

The number of defaulting concerns in the sugar industry 
went up from 11 to 16 . The performance of these concerns 
was unsatisfactory as the supply of sugarcane was adversely 
affected by drought conditions, lack of irrigation facilities , 
inadequate attention to systematic and planned cane develop 
ment measures supported by appropriate cane development 
organisation , 


Advisory Services 


57 . The Advisory Services Department established at Head 
Office of the Corporation in 1973 has been rendering advice 
and guidance to new entrepreneurs and others in the techni 
cal and financial fields both in the pre - implementation and 
post- implementation stages of their projects. Besides, the 
Department gives close attention to the complex area of the 
co poration s activities relating to the rehabilitation of pro 
jec s, which run into difficulties or develop symptoms of sick 
ness for one reason or the other . An indepth analysis of the 
malaise in each case is made by financial and technical ex 
perts and corrective steps are taken or solution devised and 
adopted in concert with other financial institutions/banks in 
volved in the project . 


Nominee Directors 

58 . An important feature in the building -up of relationship 
between IFCI and the management of assisted concerns is 
the appointment of its nominees on their boards of directors . 
In pursuance of Section 25 ( 2 ) of the IFC Act, the Corpora 
tion , as a matter of policy , reserves for itself the right to 
appoint two directors on the board of an industrial concern 
assisted by it. In the case of joint financing, the practice has 
also emerged of having one or more common nominees of 
the participating institutions, where agreed upon , 

IFCI is exercising the right to nominee its representatives 
on the boards of all assisted concerns where substantial finan 
cial assistance has been sanctioned and / or where the condi 
tions for conversion of loans into equity have been stipulated

RESOURCES 
62 . The internal resources of the Corporation for mceting 
its commitments in respect of disbursement of assistance 
sanctioned and repayment of borrowirigs from the Central 
Government and foreign credit institutions is also redeniption 
of bonds from year to year comprisc mainly additions to 
reserves , repayment of loans by assisted concerns , sale of 
investment and redemption of debentures / preference shares ; 
other sources for mceting the said commitments are borrow 
ings from the market by way of Bond Issucs and borrowings 
from foreign credit institutions. There has been no budge 
tary allocation to the Corporation from the Central Govern 
ment for the last 6 ycars ( except small amounts on account 
of interest differential funds arising out of KiW lines of credit 
and Amounts made available under the Hotel Development 
Schema ) . The Corporation has had . however , 10 reva:v 
Central Government loans obtained earlier as also loans 
raised from foreign credit institutions and has had to redeem 


honds issued in the past, according to fixed schedules and 
the quantum of such repayments are increasing year by year . 
The annual increase in internal resources and the net in 
crease in borrowing have thus not beer adequatc to support 
any substantial increase in disbursements from year to year . 
As such , the growth of disbursement of assistanco is largely 
circumscribed by the extent of market borrowings that may 
be permitted to the Corporation from year to year . Al 
though during the year under review , the Corporation was 
able to secure for thc first time, ad -hoc accommodation from 
LIC as mentioned carlier and accordingly could make addi 
tional disbursements therefron , the Corporation faced con 
xiderable stringency in regard to resources in view of the in 
creased demand for funds vis - a -vis restricted market borrow 
ings.

the Corporation stood at Rs. 10 crores as on Juñic 30 , 1975 ,

shares of the Corporation held hy the various categories of 
shareholders during the year under report .

gonc into voluntary liquidation and the Corporation is not likely to roalise the full amount invosted . No specific provi 
sion has been made in respect thereof .

Louns and Advanços do not include Rs. 9 , 09 , 38 ,000 /- in respect of which the rights and interests of the Corporation have 
been transferred under $ . 21B of the Act against amount received in cash . 


4 . An aggregaçe anlount of Rs. 340 , 77 ,850 /- was due on the date of the Balance Sheet from certain coal and coke mining 

and textile companies , the findertakings of which tiave boon acquired by the Central Governments . It has not boen 
possible to determine as to what portion of the said amount can be recovered either out of the compensation or from the 
guarantors . After taking into account tho further amount of Rs. 50 ,00 , 000 /- transferred to Reserve for Doubtful Debts 
this year , it is considered that the Riserve for Doubtful Dobts and the amount standing to the credit of Interest lield in 
Suspons9 A , . are sulient of net of tax basis to cover the doubtful loans, advances and Sundry Debtors , 


In accordance with tho past practice, tho figures of forcign currency louns availed and sub - loans granted to sub -borrowers 
are arrived at by conversion into Rupees of all foreign curroncies except pound sterling at the erstwhile IMF parity rates 
viz ., $ 1 .00 -Rs. 7 . 50, DM 48 . 80 - Rs. 100 /-, FF 1. 00 = Rs. 1 :35. The rupeo equivalent of pound sterling is arrived 
at by converting the samo at tho composite rato of £1 .00 - R3, 19 .06 , If the foreign currency balancos arc converted

TO THE SHAREHOLDERS 

OF THE INDUSTRIAL FINANCE CORPORATION OF INDIA 


We, the undersigned Auditors of the Industrial Finance Corporation of India, do horoby report to tho Shareholders upon the 
Balance Shect and Accounts of the Corporation as at 30th June, 1975. 


Wo have examined the attached Balance Sheet with the Accounts and Vouchers relating thereto and the audited returns from the 
Branches , which returns are incorporated in the above Balance Sheet , and report that whoro we have called for explanations and to 
formation , such information and explanations have been given and have been satisfactory. In our opinion , the Balance Sheet togother 
with the notos theroon is a full and fair Balanco Shoot containing all nocossary particulars and proporly drawn up in accordance with the 
Act and tho Rulos of the Corporation so as to exhibit a true and correct viow of the stato of the affairs of the Corporation accordlog to 
the best of our information and explanations given to us and as shown by the books of the Corporation .

APPENDIX H 
TERMS OF CONCESSIONAL FINANCE 

(As on June 30 , 1975 ) 


With a view to providing greater inducement to entrepreneurs to spread out in relatively under-developed areas in thc country , 
the Industrial Finance Corporation of India (IFCI), in supersession of its carlier announcements made in July 1970 and January 1972, 
has decidod to liberalise and cnlarge the scope of the scheme of financialassistance on concessionalterms to industrial projects in slich 
areas. The schem : would now cover all industrial projects - new , expansion or rehabilitation in the corporate as well as cooperative 
sectors, irrespoctive of thoir capital cost. The principal features of the revised schemeof concessional finance for units in identified less 
d - valopod districts/ areas would be 4s under : 
1. Location : 

Allindustrial projects located to be located in the districts in the various States or Union Territories selected for such assistance 
by the Central Government from time to time would be eligible for assistance on concessional terms. 
2 . Scope of the Scheme : 

Concessional finance would be extended to allindustrial projects - new and expansion both in the corporate (limited companies ) 
as woll as cooperative sectors , Concessional termswould also be made applicable to assistance granted by the Corporation by way of 
rohabilitation finance to units located in notificd less developed districts /arcas on the same basis as applicable to new and expansion 
projects . 
3 . Ceiling on Assistance : 

[ 1 , 17 :2!1 ceiling in respect of lo .115 erlended on concessional terms from the Corporation individually , unless other institutions 
al3 ) artizipitoard ortand cossional finance upto Rs. 2 . 00 crores on a pro rata basis , has been fixed at Rs. 1 . 00 crore . The sum of 
Rs. 1 .3 ) cry ? w . uld , however , include outstanding rupee assistance , if any , already granted on concessional terms. Underwriting 
assistan fron the term financing institutions including the Corporation would bemade available at concessional terms upto a ceiling of 
Rs. 1 .00 crore in the aggregate , irrespective of the cost of the project. 
4 . Terms : 

(1) Rate of Interest 

As against the normal current rate of interest on rupce loans at 11 : 25 % and on forcign currency loans at 11 -50 % (with a rebate 
of 1 % for punctual payments of instalments of interest and principal) lower rato of interest viz ., 9 . 50 % and 10 - 50 % respectively (with 
a rebate of 1 % ) would be charged under the scheme. 

(il) Period of repayment of loans 

The Corporation s normal practice is to allow initial moratorium upto 3 years to an assisted concern before the first repayment 
of the principala 

nant of the loan com :ncus. In the case of undertakings in the less developed districts/areas, this period would be 
oxtended upto five years from the date of first disbursement of the loan , on a casc to case basis , having regard to the projections of 
profitability and ways and means position of a concern . Likewise ,against thc normalperiod allowed for repayment ofloans , the period 
in the case of projects coming up in loss developed districts/areasmay be extended on themerits of cach case having regard to the concern s 
proAtability potential and cash flow position , 

( III) Promoters contribution and equity -debt ratio 

A lowor contribution than usual by promoters to the total project cost and somewhat liberal equity -debt ratio would be con 
sidered on ths merits of each cazo having regard to the financial status and standing of the promoters, gestation period of a particular 
project, its profitability potential and other relevant factors , 

( iv ) Participation in equity and preference capital 

Depending on the merits of each caso , the Corporation would be prepared to consider participation by way of underwriting or 
otherwise in the share capital of an industrial concern located in 1935 d ovalopod districts /arcas to a greater extent as compared to projects 
located elsewhere . 

(v) Reduction in other charges 

In the case of Rupee Loans, 50 % reduction would bemade in the Corporation s normal charges in respect of commitment charge . 
non - refundable examination fee for processing of applications and legal charges . Fifty por cont roduction would also be made in the 
Corporation s normal charges in respect of underwriting commission ,
